They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. PROCESSING | Builds stimulus-aligned trial-by-time neural arrays. |\n| `load_target_behavior` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Loads wheel and video-derived behavioral traces. |\n| `get_behavior_per_interval` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Interpolates behavior traces into trial-aligned fixed-length windows. |\n| `bin_behaviors` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Creates per-trial behavior targets and prefers left whisker motion energy with right fallback. |\n| `prepare_data` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | End-to-end session loader used by the methods-paper pipeline. |\n| `align_spike_behavior` | `align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`, and `binsize=0.02`.\n- The reference code is electrophysiology-only. No imaging-specific `dF/F` processing is relevant here.\n- The papers emphasize `75,708` well-isolated neurons. The raw release cluster labels (`label >= 1`) sum to exactly `75,708`, so this is the defensible neural-unit policy for this conversion.\n- The reference code exposes QC labels but does not force them in `prepare_data`; I initially mirrored that behavior, then revised to good-unit filtering after verifying the paper-level count from raw data and after the all-cluster version produced mismatched 10 s`\n- Wheel speed is the magnitude of wheel velocity.\n- Whisker motion energy is taken from the left camera when available, otherwise the right camera.\n- The executable reference code uses only: `clusters.metrics.pqt` rows with `label >= 1`.\n- Merge probes within a session after per-probe good-unit filtering.\n- Keep per-neuron Beryl-region labels for all retained neurons.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Exclude trials missing `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`, or `goCue_times`.\n- Exclude trials with reaction time wheel or whisker coverage over the stimulus-aligned `[-0.5, 1.5]` s window.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| | |\n| Choice | No exact stimulus-aligned categorical accuracy Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| Good-unit `spikes.times` and `spikes.clusters` from all probes in session | `neural` | Merge probes, filter to `label >= 1`, bin counts at 20 ms over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s, store as `(n_neurons, 100)` | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` | Counts are compact-stored as `uint8` to keep the full pickle trainable; the decoder converts them during training. |\n| Fixed stimulus-aligned time grid | `input[0]` | Time since stimulus onset, repeated identically for every trial | reference interpolation grid | Range is `[-0.48, 1.5]` because the grid stores bin-end times. |\n| Trial-table `probabilityLeft` block structure | `input[1]` | Trial number within the current block, repeated across 100 bins | task-specific derivation | Computed on the raw trial order before filtering. |\n| Trial-table `choice` | `output[0]` | `+1 -> 0` (left), `-1 -> 1` (right), repeated across time | `bin_behaviors` plus verified sign convention | No-choice trials excluded. |\n| Trial-table `probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` | `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`, repeated across time | `bin_behaviors` | This is the requested prior output. |\n| Wheel timestamps + position | `output[2]` | Interpolate absolute wheel velocity to the stimulus-aligned 20 ms grid, then discretize into 3 global tertile bins | `load_target_behavior`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Global edges are stored in metadata. |\n| Left/right whisker motion energy | `output[3]` | Per-neuron Beryl acronyms | `brain_region_idx` | Index into global `brain_regions` list | region mapping helpers | One region index per neuron. |\n| Session subject labels | `subject_idx` | Index into sorted unique subject list | release metadata | Session order matches `metadata['session_eids']`. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Good-unit policy**: Use `label >= 1` units, not all clusters, because the raw release reproduces the paper’s `75,708` well-isolated-unit count exactly under this filter.\n2. **Session retention**: Keep sessions with at least 2 valid trials after neural, wheel, and whisker filtering.\n3. **Stimulus alignment**: Use stimulus onset for all variables.\n4. **Time resolution**: Use 20 ms bins and 100 bins per trial.\n5. **Behavior discretization**: Use global tertile edges so wheel and whisker bins have consistent semantics across sessions.\n6. **Output representation**: Make all outputs time-varying so the decoder sees a uniform `(n_output, n_timepoints)` target structure.\n7. **Storage format**: Store neural counts as `uint8`, inputs as `float16`, and categorical outputs as `uint8` to keep the full dataset small enough to train.\n\n### Planned Sanity @globalize\n    def compute_spike_count(interval):\n        # We use interval_idx to track the interval order while working with p.imap_unordered()\n        interval_idx, t_beg, t_end = interval\n _, idxs_tmp, _ = np.intersect1d(cluster_ids, cluster_idxs, return_indices=True)\n        return binned_spikes_tmp[:, :n_bins], idxs_tmp, interval_idx\n\n    binned_spikes = np.zeros((n_intervals, mice rotate a wheel to indicate the location of a visual stimulus, defined as their choice (Figure 1A). For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability; in subsequent trials, it appears predominantly on one side in blocks. Mice adapt their behavior to these changing probabilities, allowing estimation of each mouse’s trial-by-trial prior belief (prior) about stimulus location. Additionally, wheel speed and whisker motion energy near the whisker pad are recorded. Whisker motion energy is computed as the mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames within a bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye. Choice and prior are static within a trial, while wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz. See STAR Methods sections “data processing” and “hyperparameter selection” for details.\nmethods.txt:13:For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset. For prior, we also align trials to the stimulus onset, including neural activity from 0.6 s to 0.1 s pre-onset. The prior represents the mice’s estimate of the stimulus side probability. We use the same decoding window as in the previous study,27 focusing on the period with minimal wheel movements. Within each trial, we segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins. For each time bin, we bin spike counts using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5,77 from each session. For dynamic behaviors (wheel speed and whisker motion energy), we select the first movement onset as the alignment event, and decode the behavior starting at the alignment event and ending at 1 s after the alignment event. The neural activity within each trial is binned into non-overlapping 20 ms bins. For each time bin, we similarly bin spike counts using all neurons from each session.\nmethods.txt:21:We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female) on the International Brain Laboratory (IBL) decision-making task (Fig. 1a,b). On each trial, a visual stimulus appeared to the left or right on a screen, and the mouse had to move it to the centre by turning a wheel with its front paws within 60 s (Fig. 1c). After an initial 90 unbiased trials, the prior probability for the stimulus to appear on the left or right side was constant over a block of trials at a ratio of 20:80% (right block) or 80:20% (left block). Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials, which were drawn from a truncated geometric distribution (empirical mean of 51 trials). Block changes were not cued. Stimulus contrast was uniformly sampled from 5 possible values (100, 25, 12.5, 6.25 and 0%). The 0% contrast trials, when no stimulus was presented, were assigned to a left or right side following the probability distribution of the block. This allowed mice to perform above chance by incorporating this prior in their choices. Following a wheel turn, mice received positive feedback in the form of a water reward, or negative feedback in the form of a white-noise pulse and a 2-s time out. The next trial began after a delay, followed by a quiescence period during which the mice had to hold the wheel still.\nmethods.txt:25:In these mice, we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes (see an example of one recording of three trials in Fig. 1i), following a grid that covered the left hemisphere of the forebrain and midbrain, which typically represent stimuli or actions on the contralateral side, and the right hemisphere of the cerebellum and hindbrain, which typically represent the ipsilateral side (Fig. 2a). Recordings were collected by 12 laboratories in Europe and the USA, with most recordings using 2 simultaneous probe insertions. To ensure reproducibility, one brain location was targeted in every mouse in every laboratory, as described elsewhere25. Only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses. Data were uploaded to a central server, preprocessed and shared through a standardized interface. To perform spike sorting on the recordings, we used a version of Kilosort with custom additions. This process produced 621,733 units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe. Dictionary with keys ['depths', 'times', 'clusters', 'amps']\n    selected_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\n        Information of clusters for this pid \n    sampling_freq: float\n        Sampling frequency of spiking data (action potential)\n    \"\"\"\n    eid = kwargs.pop('eid', '')\n    pname = kwargs.pop('pname', '')\n    spike_loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\n    sampling_freq = spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band=\"ap\", stream=True).fs\n    \n    spikes, clusters, channels = spike_loader.load_spike_sorting()\n    clusters_labeled = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\n        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=compute_metrics).to_df()\n    if qc is None:\n        return spikes, clusters_labeled, sampling_freq\n    else:\n        iok = clusters_labeled['label'] >= qc\n        selected_clusters = clusters_labeled[iok]\n        spike_idx, ib = ismember(spikes['clusters'], selected_clusters.index)\n        selected_clusters.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)\n        selected_spikes = {k: v[spike_idx] for k, v in spikes.items()}\n        selected_spikes['clusters'] = selected_clusters.index[ib].astype(np.int32)\n        return selected_spikes, selected_clusters, sampling_freq\n\n\ndef merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list):\n    \"\"\"\n    Merge spikes and clusters information from several probes as if they were recorded from the same probe.\n    This can be used to account for the fact that data from the probes recorded in the same session are not\n    statistically independent as they have the same underlying behaviour.\n\n    NOTE: The clusters dataframe will be re-indexed to avoid duplicated indices. Accordingly, spikes['clusters']\n    will be updated. To unambiguously identify clusters use the column 'uuids'\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    spikes_list: list of dicts\n        List of spike dictionaries as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader or brainwidemap.load_good_units\n    clusters_list: list of pandas.DataFrames\n        List of cluster dataframes as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters or brainwidemap.load_good_units\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    merged_spikes: dict\n        Merged and time-sorted spikes in single dictionary, where 'clusters' is adjusted to index into merged_clusters\n    merged_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\n        Merged clusters in single dataframe, re-indexed to avoid duplicate indices.\n        To unambiguously identify clusters use the column 'uuids'\n    \"\"\"\n\n    assert (len(clusters_list) == len(spikes_list)), 'clusters_list and spikes_list must have the same length'\n    assert all([isinstance(s, dict) for s in spikes_list]), 'spikes_list must contain only dictionaries'\n    assert all([isinstance(c, pd.DataFrame) for c in clusters_list]), 'clusters_list must contain only pd.DataFrames'\n\n    merged_spikes = []\n    merged_clusters = []\n    cluster_max = 0\n\n    for clusters, spikes in zip(clusters_list, spikes_list):\n        spikes['clusters'] += cluster_max\n        cluster_max = clusters.index.\n\n================================================================================\n\nSTEP 628 agent\nExecuted exec_command call_0HjAPIn4Bmnb4VCvEq4J6Jwq\nCommand: /bin/bash -lc \"sed seconds\n  - nominal `interval_len=2` seconds\n- The reference decoding paper code is built around electrophysiology, not imaging. There is no delta-F/F computation path to match.\n- Probe data are merged within a session before decoding. This implies our target format should also treat each session as a merged multi-probe session.\n- Trial curation in the reference loader:\n  - exclude trials with `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > 2 s`\n  - exclude trials with `feedback_times - goCue_times > 10 s`\n  - exclude trials with NaNs in `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n  - default call in `prepare_data` does not exclude `choice == 0` no-response trials and does not exclude the initial unbiased block\n- Neural curation in the published code is subtle:\n  - `load_spiking_data` supports QC-based filtering, but `prepare_data` calls it with `qc=None`, so all clusters are loaded\n  - metadata still records `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`, meaning cluster quality is tracked even when not filtered out\n- Continuous behavior streams are aligned by interpolation There is no delta-F/F computation path to match.\n- Probe data are merged within a session before decoding. This implies our target format should also treat each session as a merged multi-probe session.\n- Trial curation in the reference loader:\n  - exclude trials with `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > 2 s`\n  - exclude trials with `feedback_times - goCue_times > 10 s`\n  - exclude trials with NaNs in `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n  - default call in `prepare_data` does not exclude `choice == 0` no-response trials and does not exclude the initial unbiased block\n- Neural curation in the published code is subtle:\n  - `load_spiking_data` supports QC-based filtering, but `prepare_data` calls it with `qc=None`, so all clusters are loaded\n  - metadata still records `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`, meaning before masking, so filtered-out trials do not renumber experimental history.\n12. **Discretization policy**: Use dataset-wide tertile cut points for wheel speed and whisker motion energy, computed
